Compactness for the Biot pore pressures.
Next, we pass to the limit in the approximate pore pressure
p_{N}
using a compactness criterion for piecewise constant functions, given by Dreher and Jungel (see Theorem 1 in
[
34
]
). In particular, we have the following compactness result:

Proposition 5.6
.

Along a subsequence,
p_{N}\to p
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
.

Proof.

Using the chain of embeddings:

H^{1}(\Omega_{b})\subset\subset L^{2}(\Omega_{b})\subset V_{p}^{\prime}

where
V_{p}^{\prime}
is the dual of the pore pressure function space
V_{p}
defined in (
43
). Since we already have the uniform bound that
\|p_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))}\leq C
independently of
N
, to establish the desired compactness result via the Dreher-Jungel compactness criterion, it suffices to show that

\left\|\frac{\tau_{\Delta t}p_{N}-p_{N}}{\Delta t}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Delta t,T;V_{p}^{\prime})}\leq C

uniformly in the time discretization parameter
N
, where
\tau_{\Delta t}f(t,\cdot)=f(t-\Delta t,\cdot)
for
t\in[\Delta t,T]
is a time shift by
\Delta t
. In the semidiscrete formulation (
54
), we can take the test functions
\boldsymbol{\psi}
,
\boldsymbol{v}
, and
\varphi
to all be zero, which leaves the following semidiscrete weak formulation for the approximate Biot pore pressure functions, which holds for all pore pressure test functions
r\in V_{p}
:

c_{0}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\frac{p^{n+1}_{N}-p^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}r-\alpha\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N})^{\delta}\cdot\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}r-\alpha\int_{\Gamma}\left(\left(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right)r\\
+\kappa\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}p^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}r-\int_{\Gamma}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N})^{\delta}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right]r=0.

We will hence estimate
\displaystyle\left\|\frac{p^{n+1}_{N}-p^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right\|_{V_{p}^{\prime}}
using the definition of the dual norm in
V_{p}^{\prime}
and the uniform bounds on the approximate solutions. In particular, we have the following estimates for any
r\in V_{p}
with
\|r\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
on the terms in the semidiscrete weak formulation for the pore pressure:

•

Term 1.
First, we recall the definitions of
\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}
and
\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}r
from (
21
) and (
22
), and the uniform geometric bounds in (
63
). Since
\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})}
is uniformly bounded independently of
n
and
N
, by the test function estimate
\|r\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
and properties of spatial convolution,

\sup_{r\in V_{p},\|r\|_{V_{p}}\leq 1}\left|\alpha\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N})^{\delta}\cdot\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}r\right|\leq C,\qquad\text{ independently of $n$ and $N$}.

•

Term 2.
Next, we recall the definition of the rescaled normal vector from (
50
). Note that
\|\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\|\leq C
for a constant
C
independent of
n
and
N
, by the pointwise bound of
\partial_{z}\omega^{n}_{N}
in
C(0,T;C(\Gamma))
, arising from the uniform bound of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
and Sobolev embedding. Since
\|r\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
, and
\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})}\leq C
independently of
n
and
N
, we have the uniform bound (independent of
n
and
N
):

\sup_{r\in V_{p},\|r\|_{V_{p}}\leq 1}\left|\alpha\int_{\Gamma}\left(\left(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right)r\right|\leq C,

which we note depends potentially on
h
but is independent of
N
due to the uniform bound of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
(uniform in
N
, depending on
h
).

•

Term 3.
Similarly to Term 1, we obtain the uniform estimate using (
64
):

\sup_{r\in V_{p},\|r\|_{V_{p}}\leq 1}\left|\kappa\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}p^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}r\right|\leq C\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})},\ \ \text{independently of $n$ and $N$}.

•

Term 4.
Finally, for the last term, we recall from Proposition
5.4
that we have a uniform estimate (independent of
N
) on
\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}
. So similarly to Term 2, we estimate (independently of
n
and
N
) that:

\sup_{r\in V_{p},\|r\|_{V_{p}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Gamma}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N})^{\delta}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right]r\right|\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\right),

where we recall the definition of the rescaled normal vector (
50
).

Hence, combining these estimates, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\tau_{\Delta t}p_{N}-p_{N}}{\Delta t}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Delta t,T;V_{p}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|\frac{p^{n+1}_{N}-p^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right\|_{V_{p}^{\prime}}\leq C(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}+\|p_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}^{2}\right)\leq C,

by the definition of the approximate solutions
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
and
p_{N}
, the uniform bounds of
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
and
p_{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))
, independently of
N
, see Proposition
5.3
and Proposition
5.4
.
∎

Compactness for the Biot/plate velocities.
Next, we use the Dreher-Jungel compactness criterion to show a similar convergence result for the approximate Biot and plate velocities. Since the Biot and plate velocities are coupled by the regularized kinematic coupling condition, namely
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N}|_{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N}
by (
55
) and the definitions of the approximate solutions in (
70
) and (
71
), we must consider them as a pair for the following compactness arguments.

Proposition 5.7
.

Along a subsequence in
N
, and for
0<s<1
,

(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})\to(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\omega}),\qquad\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{-s}(\Omega_{b})\times H^{-s}(\Gamma)).

Proof.

We recall the definition of the space
V_{d}
from (
42
), and we define the following Biot and plate velocity test space:

\mathcal{Q}_{v}=\{(\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{\varphi})\in V_{d}\times H^{2}(\Gamma):\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}|_{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\varphi}\},

(74)

endowed with the
H^{1}(\Omega_{b})\times H^{2}(\Gamma)
norm, where we emphasize that the regularized kinematic coupling condition is incorporated into the definition of the test space
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
. We can then observe the following chain of embeddings:

L^{2}(\Omega_{b})\times L^{2}(\Gamma)\subset\subset H^{-s}(\Omega_{b})\times H^{-s}(\Gamma)\subset\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime}.

To apply the Dreher-Jungel compactness criterion (Theorem 1 in
[
34
]
) and conclude the desired convergence result, it suffices to establish the following estimate (independently of
N
):

\left\|\frac{\tau_{\Delta t}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})-(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})}{\Delta t}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Delta t,T;\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime})}\leq C,

since we already have a uniform bound on
\|(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})\times L^{2}(\Gamma)}
independently of
N
, as a result of the uniform energy estimates, see Proposition
5.3
.

So it suffices to estimate the following quantity for test functions
(\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{\varphi})\in\mathcal{Q}_{v}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
and
\|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq 1
:

\left|\int_{\Omega_{b}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}+\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right|

To do this, we set the test functions
\boldsymbol{v}
and
r
to be zero in the semidiscrete weak formulation in (
54
). Hence, we have that the approximate Biot and plate velocities satisfy the following identity for all test functions
(\boldsymbol{\psi},\varphi)\in\mathcal{Q}_{v}
:

\rho_{b}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}+\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}-p^{n+1}_{N}\right)\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}+\beta\int_{\Gamma}\Big(\mathcal{S}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{\Gamma}\Big)^{-1}\left(\left(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)^{\delta}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\left(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right)\\
+h\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\varphi}+2\mu_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})+\lambda_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}_{N})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})+2\mu_{v}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\\
+\lambda_{v}\int_{\Omega_{b}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})-\alpha\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}p^{n+1}_{N}\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}+h\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{\varphi}+h\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{\varphi}=0.

We estimate the terms for test functions
\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
and
\|\boldsymbol{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq 1
as follows:

•

Recall the definition of the rescaled normal vector in (
50
). Since
\|\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}
is uniformly bounded in
n
and
N
, we have that
\|\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}
is uniformly bounded in
n
and
N
. Hence,

\left|\int_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}-p^{n+1}_{N}\right)\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right|\leq C\left(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\right).

•

Recalling the definition in (
50
), we have that
\|\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\leq C
independently of
n
and
N
. We have the estimate
(\mathcal{S}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{\Gamma})^{-1}\leq C
independently of
n
and
N
, for the arc length element defined in (
38
) as a direct consequence of the uniform geometric estimate (
65
). By the regularized kinematic coupling condition which is enforced in the scheme in (
55
),
(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N})^{\delta}|_{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}
where we have a uniform bound on
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}
in
L^{2}(\Gamma)
. Therefore, we estimate that

\left|\beta\int_{\Gamma}\Big(\mathcal{S}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{\Gamma}\Big)^{-1}\left(\left(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)^{\delta}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\left(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\right)\right|\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\right).

•

From (
21
) and (
22
),
\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}=\text{det}\left(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n}_{N})^{\delta}\right)
and

\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}=\text{tr}\left(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n}_{N})^{\delta}\right)^{-1}\right).

Because the spatial regularization parameter
\delta
is fixed, by the regularizing properties of convolution,
\|(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n}_{N})^{\delta}\|_{H^{k}(\Omega_{b})}
is uniformly bounded independently of
n
and
N
for any positive integer
k
. Since
\|p^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})}
is uniformly bounded independently of
n
and
N
, we obtain using the uniform bounds in (
63
):

\left|\alpha\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}p^{n+1}_{N}\nabla^{(\eta^{n}_{N})^{\delta}}_{b}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}\right|\leq C,\qquad\text{ independently of $n$ and $N$}.

•

Finally, by the uniform bounds of
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}_{N}
in
H^{1}(\Omega_{b})
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}
in
H^{2}(\Gamma)
, we obtain the following estimate for the remaining terms:

\left|2\mu_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})+\lambda_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}^{n+1}_{N})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})+2\mu_{v}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\right.\\
\left.+\lambda_{v}\int_{\Omega_{b}}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})+h\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{\varphi}+h\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{\varphi}\right|\\
\begin{cases}\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)}\right),\ \ \text{ for $\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}=0$ (purely poroelastic Biot medium)},\\
\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{2}_{0}(\Gamma)}\right),\ \ \text{ for $\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}>0$ (poroviscoelastic Biot medium)}.\end{cases}

Hence, we obtain the final estimate that:

\left\|\frac{(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N})-(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n}_{N})}{\Delta t}\right\|_{\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime}}\\
\begin{cases}\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\right),\ \ \text{ for $\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}=0$},\\
\leq C\left(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{n+1}_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\right),\ \ \text{ for $\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}>0$}.\end{cases}

Therefore, summing over
n=0,1,...,N-1
, we obtain the desired estimate:

\left\|\frac{\tau_{\Delta t}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})-(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})}{\Delta t}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Delta t,T;\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime})}\\
\begin{cases}\leq 2C\left(T+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}))}^{2}+\|p_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}_{0}(\Gamma))}\right)\leq C,\ \ \text{ for $\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}=0$},\\
\leq 2C\left(T+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}))}^{2}+\|p_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))}+\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N}^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\right)\leq C,\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\text{ for }\mu_{v},\lambda_{v}>0,\end{cases}

where we used the uniform trace bound in Proposition
5.4
and the uniform dissipation estimates on the approximate solutions in Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Compactness arguments for the approximate fluid velocities.
Recall that the approximate (time-discrete) fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}
from (
54
) is defined on a fluid domain
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}:=\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}(\Omega_{f})
determined by the plate displacement
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N}
from the previous time step, so that the fluid velocities are defined on fluid domains that are changing with respect to
n
and
N
. To handle this, we will consider the fluid velocities to be defined on the maximal domain
\Omega:=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:|\boldsymbol{x}|<2\}
. Note that by the injectivity of the Lagrangian map
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}^{\omega_{N}}_{\Gamma}
and the fact that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}^{\omega_{N}}_{\Gamma}(\hat{\Gamma})\cap\partial\Omega=\varnothing
in Proposition
5.2
, we have that
\Omega
contains all of the moving time-discrete fluid domains
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}
. Then, we can define the approximate fluid velocities
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
on the maximal domain
\Omega
by using an extension by zero, and by the properties of extension by zero (see Theorem 8.2 in
[
46
]
), we have the following result:

Proposition 5.8
.

The fluid velocities
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
defined on the maximal domain
\Omega
are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
and
L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}(\Omega))
for
0<s<1/2
.

The goal is then to use the uniform bounds and the semidiscrete weak formulation to show that the fluid velocities
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
are precompact in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
, which is the content of the next proposition. To prove this result, we use an extension of the Aubin-Lions compactness lemma to sequences of functions defined on moving domains, see
[
62
]
. For this purpose, it will be easier to work with fluid velocities defined on the physical domains, and we will rewrite the semidiscrete formulation for the fluid velocity functions defined on the physical domain. To do this, we recall that
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}
is a function in the following time-discrete solution space:

V^{n}_{N}=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in H^{1}(\Omega^{n}_{f,N}):\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}=0\text{ on }\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\text{ and }\boldsymbol{u}=0\text{ on }\partial\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\setminus\Gamma^{n}_{N}\},

where

\Omega^{n}_{N}:=\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}(\Omega_{f}),\qquad\Gamma^{n}_{N}=\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma),

and we define the corresponding test space
Q^{n}_{N}
to be a more regular subspace of the solution space:

Q^{n}_{N}=V^{n}_{N}\cap H^{3}(\Omega^{n}_{N}).

(75)

We will also define the following function spaces on the entire maximal domain:

V=H^{s}(\Omega)\text{ for some }0<s<1/2,\qquad H=L^{2}(\Omega),

where by properties of extension by zero, the extension by zero of any function in
V^{n}_{N}
is in the common solution space
V
defined on the fixed maximal domain
\Omega
. We recall that because the Biot-fluid subproblem in the splitting scheme uses the previous plate displacement
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N}
to compute
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}
, we have that
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}
is defined on the (previous) fluid domain
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}
. To write the semidiscrete formulation for the approximate fluid velocity, it is hence helpful to introduce the notation:

\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}=\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\circ(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1},

(76)

which transforms the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}
on
\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}
to a fluid velocity
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}
defined on the domain
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}
. We then obtain the following semidiscrete formulation for the approximate fluid velocities defined on the physical fluid domain, which holds for all fluid velocity test functions
\boldsymbol{q}\in V^{n}_{N}
:

\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}+2\nu\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{v})-\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-p^{n+1}_{N}\right)(\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\\
+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\left[\left(\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}-\left(\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{v}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\right]\\
+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{1}{\Delta t}\left(\frac{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}}{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}}-1\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}\left(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{v})-\beta\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}(\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau})=0,

(77)

where
\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}
is the discretized (fluid domain) ALE velocity defined on the fixed fluid domain
\Omega_{f}
:

\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}(x,y)=\frac{1}{\Delta t}\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)\quad\text{ for }(x,y)\in\Omega_{f},

(78)

and on the physical fluid domain:

\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N}=\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\circ\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)^{-1}.

(79)

We can use this semidiscrete weak formulation and the Aubin-Lions compactness criterion for functions defined on moving domains
[
62
]
to obtain the following strong convergence result for the approximate fluid velocities
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
extended by zero to the maximal domain
\Omega
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Along a subsequence in
N
,
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{u}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
.

This proposition will follow from an Aubin-Lions compactness result
[
62
]
for functions defined on moving domains as in the proof of Proposition 8.3 in
[
48
]
, once we verify the following essential condition, referred to in the proof of Proposition 8.3 in
[
48
]
as
Generalized Property B
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
P^{n}_{N}:H\to\overline{Q^{n}_{N}}^{H}
denote the orthogonal projection operator, where
\overline{Q^{n}_{N}}^{H}
denotes the topological closure of
Q^{n}_{N}\subset H
with respect to the topology of strong convergence in
H
. Then, the following estimate holds:

\left\|P^{n}_{N}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right\|_{(Q^{n}_{N})^{\prime}}\leq C\left(a^{n}_{N}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\right)^{3/2},

for nonnegative constants
\{a^{n}_{N}\}
satisfying
(\Delta t)\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}|a^{n}_{N}|^{2}\leq c
, where the constants
c
and
C
are independent of
n
and
N
.

The strategy to establish Generalized Property B in Lemma
5.1
is to use
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}
defined in (
76
) as an intermediate step, by estimating the following quantity for test functions
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1
:

\left|\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|+\left|\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|.

(80)

The first term can be estimated using the semidiscrete formulation (
54
), which will be the content of the proof of Lemma
5.1
below. We will estimate the second term using the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a constant
C
that is independent of
N
such that for the test space
Q^{n}_{N}
defined in (
75
):

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|\leq C\Big(a^{n}_{N}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2},

for nonnegative constants
\{a^{n}_{N}\}
satisfying
\displaystyle(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}|a^{n}_{N}|^{2}\leq c
for constants
c
and
C
that are independent of
n
and
N
.

Proof.

Let us define
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}:=\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}
. Then, consider an arbitrary
\boldsymbol{q}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1
with
Q^{n}_{N}
defined in (
75
), so that
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{n}_{f,N})}\leq C
. Recalling the definition of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}
from (
76
), we estimate:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\Big(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\Big)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega_{f}}\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{A_{1}}+\int_{A_{2}}\right)\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|,

\displaystyle\ \ \text{ for }A_{1}:=(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}(\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}\cap\Omega^{n}_{f,N})\text{ and }A_{2}:=\Omega_{f}\setminus A_{1}.

Estimate of integral over
A_{1}
.
We estimate the integral over
A_{1}
, where we recall that
\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}
is defined on
\Omega^{n-1}_{N,f}
(and extended by zero outside). By the definition of
A_{1}
, both
(x,y)
and
\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)
are in
\Omega_{f}
if
(x,y)\in A_{1}
. So let us define the polar coordinates of
(x,y)
and
\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)
to be the
(r_{1},\theta_{1})
and
(r_{2},\theta_{2})
for some
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(-2\pi,2\pi)
, see the Appendix and the discussion preceding Proposition
8.1
. We then define as in the Appendix, the curves in polar coordinates for
0\leq s\leq 1
:

\gamma_{1}:s\to((1-s)r_{1}+sr_{2},\theta_{1}),\qquad\gamma_{2}:s\to(r_{2},(1-s)\theta_{1}+s\theta_{2}).

See Figure
3
in the Appdendix. Thus, for
(x,y)\in A_{1}\subset\Omega_{f}:=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:1<|\boldsymbol{x}|<2\}
, we can use the fundamental theorem of line integrals to estimate:

\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|\leq\int_{\gamma_{1}(x,y)}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\cdot d\boldsymbol{r}+\int_{\gamma_{2}(x,y)}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\cdot d\boldsymbol{r}\\
\leq\Big|(x,y)-\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)\Big|\left(\int_{1}^{2}|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}(r,\theta_{1})|dr+\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=r_{2}}|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}|ds\right).

(81)

Note that

|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}(x,y)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}(x,y)|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}(\cdot)\|_{H^{3/2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)},

and hence, by Proposition
8.2
and the uniform bounds (
64
):

\Big|(x,y)-\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)\Big|\leq\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{f})}\cdot|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}(x,y)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}(x,y)|\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}.

(82)

We also compute by a change of variables and the uniform geometric bounds in (
64
):

\displaystyle\int_{A_{1}}\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=r_{2}}

\displaystyle|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}|dsdxdy

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{1}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big|\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)\Big|\cdot\Bigg|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\Big(\Big|\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(x,y)\Big|,\theta\Big)\Bigg|d\theta dxdy

\displaystyle\leq\text{det}\Big(\nabla\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\circ(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\Big)\Big)\int_{\Omega_{f}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|(x,y)|\Big|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}(|(x,y)|,\theta)\Big|d\theta dxdy

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega_{f}}\int_{|x|=(x,y)}|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}|dsdxdy,

(83)

where by the cofactor formula for the matrix inverse, the uniform lower bound on
\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}
and the upper bound on
|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}|
in (
64
) give a uniform upper bound on
|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}|
. Hence, by integrating (
81
) over
A_{1}
(which is bounded by its integral over the entire
\Omega_{f}
), by applying (
82
), and by using the coarea formula (see
[
35
]
)
\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{f}}h=\int_{1}^{2}\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=r}hdsdr
, along with the uniform geometric bounds in Proposition
5.2
and (
5.3
):

\displaystyle\int_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\left(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{1}^{2}|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}(\tilde{r},\theta)|rd\tilde{r}d\theta dr+\int_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}(r,\tilde{\theta})|rd\tilde{\theta}d\theta dr\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}.

(84)

Estimate of integral over
A_{2}
.
By the definition of
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, we note that for
\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}
defined on
\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}
and extended by zero outside, we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}|_{A_{2}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, so by a change of variables:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{2}}\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{2}}\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\int_{\left(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\right)^{-1}(\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\setminus\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N})}\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big|.

(85)

Let
\gamma(z;s)
for
z\in\Gamma
be the line connecting
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n-1}_{N}(z)
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N}(z)
, parametrized for fixed
z\in\Gamma
by:

\gamma(x;s)=s\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{\Gamma}(z)+(1-s)\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{\Gamma}(z),\qquad 0\leq s\leq 1,

which has a length as a curve contained in the maximal domain
\Omega
of

\text{Length}(\gamma(z;\cdot))\leq\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N}(z)-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n-1}_{N}(z)=(\Delta t)\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}(z)\leq c_{0}(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}.

(86)

Therefore, every point in
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\setminus\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}
is a distance of less than or equal to
c_{0}(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}
away from
\Gamma^{n}_{f,N}
. Furthermore, every point in
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\setminus\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}
can be connected to a point on
\Gamma^{n}_{N}
, via a curve lying entirely in
\overline{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}
of length less than or equal to
c_{0}(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}
. So by the transformation theorem for lengths in Proposition
8.2
, we conclude that the region
(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}(\Omega^{n-1}_{f,N}\cap\Omega^{n}_{f,N})
is contained within the thin annulus:

D_{\epsilon}:=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}:1<|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq 1+\epsilon\},\qquad\text{ for }\epsilon=c_{0}(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\cdot\|\nabla(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{n}_{f,N})}.

See Figure
2
for a visual representation.

Figure 2:
An illustration of the integral over
A_{2}
and the associated domains in both the moving domain (left) and the reference domain (right) configuration, transformed by the map
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}
.

Thus, from (
5.3
), Proposition
5.2
and the bounds (
64
), and the Dirichlet boundary condition that
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}=0
on
|\boldsymbol{x}|=2
:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{2}}\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1}^{1+\epsilon}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}(r,\theta)|rdrd\theta

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1}^{1+\epsilon}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left|\int_{r}^{2}\partial_{r}\Big(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(\tilde{r},\theta)d\tilde{r}\right|d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1}^{1+\epsilon}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{1}^{2}\Big|\nabla\Big(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)(\tilde{r},\theta)\Big|d\tilde{r}d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{1}^{1+\epsilon}\Big\|\nabla\Big(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\cdot\Big\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\circ(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n-1}_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\Big\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}.

(87)

Conclusion of proof.
Combining (
5.3
) and (
5.3
), we obtain

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2},

where we can set
a^{n}_{N}:=1+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}
in the statement of the lemma, since
\displaystyle(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}|a^{n}_{N}|^{2}\leq c
as a result of
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*}_{N}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
and hence
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*}_{N}\in L^{4}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))
.
∎

Proof of Generalized Property B, Lemma
5.1
.

We consider a fluid test function
\boldsymbol{v}\in Q^{n}_{N}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1
. Then, by the triangle inequality estimate in (
80
) and the estimate in Lemma
5.2
, it suffices to estimate the following quantity

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|.

We can estimate this, using the semidiscrete formulation for the fluid in (
77
), which gives rise to the following terms:

Term 1.
We estimate, by using
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega)}\leq 1
along with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, that
\displaystyle\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{q})\right|\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}
. Here, we recall the definition of the more regular test space
Q^{n}_{N}
in (
75
), which gives us an
H^{3}(\Omega)
estimate on the fluid test function
\boldsymbol{q}
.

Term 2.
Since
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1
, by the trace inequality,
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C
for a constant
C
independent of
n
and
N
, by uniform bounds on the ALE fluid domain map as in Proposition
5.4
. Hence, we can estimate using Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|q\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-p^{n+1}_{N}\right)(\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\boldsymbol{n})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{3/4}(\Omega_{f})}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{H^{3/4}(\Omega_{f})}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\Big).

By using the uniform bound on
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))
and interpolation, we continue the chain of inequalities as:

\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|^{3/4}_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|^{3/4}_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\Big)\leq C\Big(1+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}.

Term 3.
Before estimating Term 3, we derive an estimate for the discretized (fluid domain) ALE velocity
\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N}
in (
79
). We note that on the fixed domain,
\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}:=(\Delta t)^{-1}\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)
defined in (
78
) satisfies the harmonic equation
\Delta\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}=0
on
\Omega_{f}

with boundary conditions

\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}=\boldsymbol{0}\text{ on }\partial\Omega_{f}\setminus\Gamma,\quad\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}=(\Delta t)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\omega}^{n}_{N})=\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1/2}_{N}\text{ on }\Gamma.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*}_{N}
defined in (
72
) is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
and is hence in
L^{4}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))
by uniform estimates, so therefore we can estimate,

\|\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}.

Thus, since
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1
, we can use the uniform bounds on the Jacobian of the fluid ALE map in (
64
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|q\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\Bigg|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}

\displaystyle[((\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N})\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}-((\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}^{n+1}_{N})\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{q})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}+\|\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}^{3}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}^{3/2}\Big)\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{w}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}.

Term 4.
We estimate the following term:

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{1}{\Delta t}\left(\frac{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}}{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}}-1\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|

By the uniform bounds on the ALE map for the fluid domain in (
64
), we have that
\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\geq c>0
for some positive constant
c
. We compute that

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}

\displaystyle=\Big(\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,x}\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,y}-\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,x}\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,y}\Big)-\Big(\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,y}\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,x}-\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,y}\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,x}\Big)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\Big(\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,x}-\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,x}\Big)\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,y}

\displaystyle+\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,x}\partial_{y}\Big(\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,y}-\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,y}\Big)-\partial_{x}\Big(\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,y}-\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,y}\Big)\partial_{y}\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,x}-\partial_{x}\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,y}\partial_{y}\Big(\Phi^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f,x}-\Phi^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f,x}\Big).

We recall the uniform bound on
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
from Proposition
5.3
, which implies that

\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\|_{H^{5/2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C

for a constant
C
that is independent of
n
and
N
, and furthermore

\left\|(\Delta t)^{-1}\nabla\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq\left\|(\Delta t)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f})\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}.

Therefore, we estimate that

\left\|(\Delta t)^{-1}\Big(\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\\
\leq C\Big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{f})}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{f})}\Big)\left\|(\Delta t)^{-1}\nabla\Big(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)},

and hence,

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{1}{\Delta t}\left(\frac{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n+1}_{N}}_{f}}{\mathcal{J}^{\omega^{n}_{N}}_{f}}-1\right)\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}\Big)^{3/2}.

Term 5.
Next, we estimate the term, using
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega_{f})}\leq 1
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}\cdot\boldsymbol{q})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{4}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2},

using the same interpolation estimates as in Term 2.

Term 6.
Finally, we estimate the slip term, by using the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega_{f})}\leq 1
, Sobolev embedding, and the trace theorem:

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Gamma^{n}_{N}}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}(\boldsymbol{q}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau})\right|\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\Big)\leq C\Big(1+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2}.

Conclusion.
Combining the preceding estimates, we obtain that

\sup_{\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{Q^{n}_{N}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Omega^{n}_{f,N}}\frac{\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}^{n}_{N}}{\Delta t}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\right|\leq C\Big(1+\|p^{n+1}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{N}\|_{V^{n}_{N}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}\|_{V^{n+1}_{N}}\Big)^{3/2},

which establishes Generalized Property B, since
\displaystyle(\Delta t)\sum_{n=1}^{N}a^{n}_{N}\leq C
uniformly in
N
for

a^{n}_{N}:=1+\|p^{n}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}+\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}_{N}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}^{2}

by uniform bounds in
N
of
p_{N}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))
and
\zeta^{*}_{N}\in L^{4}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
.
∎

We hence have convergence of the physical fluid velocities on the maximal fluid domain in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
. Because the semidiscrete formulation involves the fluid velocity transferred to the fixed domain, we want to obtain a corresponding convergence result for the fluid velocities
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}
on the fixed domain
\Omega_{f}
. In addition, the semidiscrete weak formulation also involves the trace of the fluid velocity along the moving interface, so we also establish the following convergence result for the traces of the fluid velocities, which follows from the strong convergence
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{u}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
.

Proposition 5.10
.

We also have the following strong convergences for the fluid velocities
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}:=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\circ\Phi^{\omega_{N}}_{f}
defined on the fixed fluid domain
\Omega_{f}
:

\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\quad\text{ strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))$},

\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}|_{\Gamma}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma},\quad\text{ strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))$}.

Proof.

By the uniform geometric bounds in Proposition
5.2
, we have that
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}
are all uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}))
independently of
N
, so it suffices to show the convergence
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))
as this implies the corresponding convergence of the traces by Lemma 9.1 in
[
48
]
. We define

\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}:=\boldsymbol{u}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f}

and estimate

\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{f}}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}|^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{f,N}(t)}\mathcal{J}^{\omega_{N}(t)}_{f}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}-\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\to 0,

where we used the fact that
\Omega^{n}_{f,N}\subset\Omega
for all
n
and
N
, the uniform geometric bounds in Proposition
5.2
, and the fact that
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{u}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
on the maximal domain as
N\to\infty
.

So it suffices to show that
\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))}^{2}\to 0
, which is a similar computation to the computation in Lemma
5.2
. Namely, we estimate

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{f}}|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A_{1}(t)}\cdot+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A_{2}(t)}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}\Big)\Big|^{2},

for

A_{1}(t):=(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}(t)}_{f})^{-1}(\Omega_{f,N}(t)\cap\Omega_{f}(t))\text{ and }A_{2}(t):=\Omega_{f}\setminus A_{1}(t),

where we are using
\Omega_{f}(t)
to denote the moving fluid domain associated with the limiting structure displacement
\boldsymbol{\omega}(t)
. To estimate the integral over
A_{1}(t)
, note that

|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f}(x,y)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}(x,y)|\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{N}(\cdot)\|_{H^{3/2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}-\boldsymbol{\omega}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}.

Then, combining this with the same calculation as in (
81
), (
82
), and (
5.3
), we estimate that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A_{1}(t)}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}\Big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}(t))}\to 0,

(88)

by the strong convergence of the plate displacements in Proposition
5.5
. To estimate the integral over
A_{2}(t)
, we use the calculation in (
86
) to conclude that the maximum radial “width” of the region
A_{2}(t)
at a coordinate of
z\in\Gamma
is less than or equal to

r_{\Delta t}(t,z):=C(\boldsymbol{\omega}(t,z)-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}(t,z))

so that by the same calculation in (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A_{2}(t)}\Big|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}\circ\Big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f})^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}\Big)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(\Gamma)}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{f}(t))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;H^{3/2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}(t)))}\to 0,

(89)

by the strong convergence in Proposition
5.5
. This completes the proof, as we can combine (
5.3
) and (
5.3
) to conclude that
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))
.
∎

5.4
Convergence of test functions and passage to the limit

The final step of the existence proof is to pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in the semidiscrete formulation (
54
) to obtain the limiting weak formulation given in Definition
5.1
. Let us summarize the strong convergences that we have so far, which will be useful for this limit passage.

Proposition 5.11

(Strong convergences)
.

We have the following
strong
convergences of the approximate solutions:

•

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{\omega}
strongly in
C(0,T;C(\Gamma))
.

•

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{\eta}
strongly in
C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
.

•

p_{N}\to p
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
.

•

(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{N})\to(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\omega})
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{-s}(\Omega_{b})\times H^{-s}(\Gamma))
for
0<s<1
.

•

\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))
.

•

\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}|_{\Gamma}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma}
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
.

Because the moving domain appears in the semidiscrete weak formulations in terms of a nonlinear geometric condition on the test functions in the test space
\mathcal{V}^{\omega_{N}}_{f}
, namely:

\nabla^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}_{f}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=0\text{ on }\Omega_{f}

where we recall the definition of
\nabla^{\omega_{N}}_{f}
from (
32
), we must also carefully pass to the limit in the fluid test functions. Recall from the Biot/fluid subproblem in (
54
) that the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}
satisfies
\nabla^{\omega^{n}_{N}}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}^{n+1}_{N}=0
on
\Omega_{f}
, in terms of the
previous
structure displacement, and hence, the time-shifted approximate structure displacement
\tau_{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
appears in the divergence-free condition:

\tau_{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}(t,\cdot)=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}(t-\Delta t,\cdot),\qquad\text{ for }\Delta t\leq t\leq T.

In contrast to other FSI problems which involve kinematic coupling between the fluid velocity and the plate velocity at the interface in terms of
\boldsymbol{u}|_{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\omega}
(see for example
[
57
]
), the fluid test functions in this FPSI problem are fully decoupled from the other test functions, which somewhat simplifies this test function construction. This was already observed for moving domain (nonlinearly geometrically coupled) FPSI problems in
[
48
]
, for example.

The goal will be to consider a target test function for the limit and then approximate the target test function by test functions for the semidiscrete formulation (
54
) which converge strongly to the target test function as
N\to\infty
. To define the
target test space
, we define the function space
\mathcal{X}
consisting of all continuously differentiable functions
\boldsymbol{v}\in C^{1}_{c}([0,T);H^{1}(\Omega))
defined on the maximal domain
\Omega
, such that the following additional conditions are satisfied:

•

Spatial smoothness for all times.

\boldsymbol{v}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

•

Divergence-free condition.

\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=0
for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\Omega
.

•

Dirichlet boundary conditions.

\boldsymbol{v}|_{\partial\Omega_{f}\setminus\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{0}
.

We will then use the ALE map to pull back the spatially smooth test functions in
\mathcal{X}
back to the fixed fluid domain
\Omega_{f}
:

\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}:=\boldsymbol{v}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f},

where
\omega
is the limiting structure displacement. We denote the set of all possible test functions
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}
obtained this way from all possible choices of
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{X}
by
\mathcal{X}^{\omega}_{f}
, which is the
target test space
. The target test space
\mathcal{X}^{\omega}_{f}
has the essential property that it is dense in the full test space
\mathcal{V}^{\omega}_{f}
defined in (
49
) for the limiting problem, so it suffices to verify that the limiting weak formulation in Definition
5.1
holds for arbitrary test functions
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\in\mathcal{X}^{\omega}_{f}
.

Using the fluid domain ALE map, we also define admissible test functions for each
N
for the corresponding semidiscrete formulation using the test function
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{X}
:

\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}=\boldsymbol{v}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}_{f},

(90)

and the key property is that
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}
are admissible test functions in the sense that
\nabla^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}_{f}\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}=0
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore, these approximate test functions
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}
converge strongly to the limiting target test function
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{X}^{\omega}_{f}
in the following sense, which (combined with the strong convergences in Proposition
5.11
and the weak convergences in Proposition
5.3
) allows us to pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in the semidiscrete formulation with the test function
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}
to obtain the limiting weak formulation with test function
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}
.

Proposition 5.12
.

For an arbitrary
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have the following convergences:

\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}\to\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\text{ and }\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}\to\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{v}},

both uniformly and pointwise on
[0,T]\times\Omega_{f}
.

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{v}
is spatially smooth, it suffices to show that for
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, chosen so that
5/2-\epsilon>2
:

\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\tau_{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;H^{2-\epsilon}(\Gamma))},

(91)

\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}-\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\tau_{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;H^{2-\epsilon}(\Gamma))},

(92)

as the result would follow by using that
\boldsymbol{v}
is Lipschitz continuous in space (with a uniform Lipschitz constant in time) and from the convergence of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{\omega}
in
C(0,T;H^{s}(\Gamma))
for all
0<s<2
. In addition, to show that
\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}-\tau_{\Delta t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;H^{2-\epsilon}(\Gamma)}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, we use the uniform bound of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
in
C^{\alpha}(0,T;H^{2-\epsilon}(\Gamma))
for some
\alpha>0
, which follows from the interpolation and the uniform bounds of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))\cap W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
.

To establish these inequalities (
91
) and (
92
), note that
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f}
satisfies
\Delta(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f})=0
. Furthermore, it is equal to
\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}
along
\Gamma
and has zero boundary data along the rest of
\partial\Omega_{f}
. Hence, by elliptic estimates, we conclude that

\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{N}}_{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{5/2-\epsilon}(\Omega_{f}))}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2-\epsilon}(\Gamma))},

which establishes the desired estimates by Sobolev embedding, since
5/2-\epsilon>2
.
∎

We can thus conclude the proof of existence of a regularized interface weak solution to the FPSI problem with a plate
h>0
, with a regularized kinematic coupling condition, in the sense of Definition
5.1
, by considering
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\in\mathcal{X}^{\omega}_{f}
in the target test space (which is dense in the full test space
\mathcal{V}^{\omega}_{f}
) and considering an approximating sequence of test functions
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}
as defined in (
90
), which are admissible test functions for the approximate semidiscrete weak formulation in (
54
), integrated over time on each subinterval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
and summed over
n=0,1,...,N-1
. We can then pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in each of the terms in the resulting approximate weak formulations using the weak and weak-star convergences in Proposition
5.3
, the strong convergences in Proposition
5.11
, and the strong convergence of the test functions
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}_{N}
to
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}
in Proposition
5.12
. We then obtain the limiting weak formulation in Definition
5.1
in the limit as
N\to\infty
. For more details on the limit passage in the approximate weak formulations, we refer the reader to Section 9.3 in
[
48
]
, and related works on prototypical incompressible FSI models in Section 7.2 of
[
57
]
and Section 2.7.2 of
[
71
]
. This establishes the existence of a regularized interface weak solution to the approximate system with a plate of thickness
h>0
, see Theorem
5.1
.

6
Passage to the limit in the plate thickness,
h\to 0

Having obtained existence of weak regularized interface solutions for the approximate problem with a plate of thickness
h>0
in the sense of Definition
5.1
, we pass to the limit in the plate thickness
h\to 0
with the objective of obtaining a weak (regularized interface) formulation for the coupled moving domain Biot-fluid problem without a plate interface separating the poroelastic Biot medium and the incompressible viscous fluid, in the sense of Definition
4.3
. Such a problem of a practical importance in applications of fluid-poroelastic structure interaction where the poroelastic medium is in direct contact with the fluid.

In the previous Section
5
, we have established existence of a regularized interface weak solution to a moving domain FPSI problem containing a plate of thickness
h>0
separating the Biot medium and the fluid, see Theorem
5.1
. To emphasize the dependence on the thickness of the plate, we denote the solution to the problem with plate thickness
h>0
by
(\boldsymbol{u}_{h},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h},p_{h},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h})
. We emphasize that the problem will still contain a regularization by
\delta
, and we will leave the parameter
\delta
implicit in the notation for notational simplicity. While we take the limit as
h\to 0
in the solutions
(\boldsymbol{u}_{h},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h},p_{h},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h})
, we keep the regularization parameter
\delta>0
fixed but arbitrary throughout the limit passage as
h\to 0
.

From the existence proof, we have that the weak solutions to the regularized FPSI problem with plate thickness
h>0
satisfy the following energy inequality, as a consequence of the weak convergences in Proposition
5.3
and weak lower-semicontinuity:

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{f}(t)}|\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{b}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\left|h^{1/2}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\right|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\left|h^{1/2}\Delta\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\left|h^{1/2}\Delta\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\right|^{2}+\mu_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h})|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}c_{0}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|p_{h}|^{2}+2\nu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{f}(t)}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{u}_{h})|^{2}+\beta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\left(\mathcal{J}_{\Gamma}^{\eta_{h}}\right)^{-1}\left|\left(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}-\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\omega_{h}}\right|^{2}\\
+2\mu_{v}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\boldsymbol{D}(\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h})|^{2}+\lambda_{v}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\nabla\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}|^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}\left|\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}p_{h}\right|^{2}\leq E_{0},

(93)

where
E_{0}
is the initial energy, defined by:

E_{0}:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{f}(0)}|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{b}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|h^{1/2}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\left|h^{1/2}\Delta\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}\right|^{2}+\mu_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{e}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}c_{0}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|p_{0}|^{2},

and the initial interface displacement
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}
is defined by
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}:=(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}|_{\Gamma}
in agreement with the regularized kinematic coupling condition.

Before passing to the limit as the plate thickness
h\to 0
, we need some uniform control on geometric quantities involved in the problem. Namely, we want uniform control over the displacement of the fluid-Biot interface (which for
h>0
is the plate displacement) to avoid collision with the boundary of
\Omega
(the entire combined domain) and geometric degeneracy. In addition, we want uniform control on the regularized Lagrangian map
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\eta^{\delta}}_{b}
for the Biot domain and the ALE map for the fluid domain
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega}_{f}
, which will allow us to convert between time-dependent and fixed Biot/fluid domains uniformly in the plate thickness parameter
h
. This is the content of the following result, where we will assume without loss of generality that
h\leq 1
, as we take the limit
h\to 0
(compare to the corresponding uniform geometric bounds in
N
for the
h
-level problem in Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
).

Proposition 6.1
.

There exists a time
T>0
independent of
0<h\leq 1
, such that there exists a regularized interface weak solution to the FPSI problem with plate thickness
h>0
on the time interval
[0,T]
. Furthermore, for this
T>0
, there exist positive constants
R_{max}
,
c_{0}
,
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
, and
c_{3}
(which are all independent of
0<h\leq 1
) such that the following geometric bounds hold uniformly in
0<h\leq 1
for all
t\in[0,T]
:

1.

Non-degeneracy of the moving interface.
The map
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}}_{\Gamma}:\Gamma\to\Gamma(t)
is injective,
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma)\cap\partial\Omega=\varnothing
for all
t\in[0,T]
, and for a positive constant
\alpha>0
that is independent of
0<h\leq 1
:

|(-\sin(z),\cos(z))+\partial_{z}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}(z)|\geq\alpha>0,\qquad\text{ for all }z\in\Gamma.

(94)

2.

Invertibility of the regularized Lagrangian map.
There exists positive constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that

\text{det}(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})\geq c_{0}>0,\ \ |(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})^{-1}|\geq c_{1}>0,\ \ |\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}|\leq c_{2}\quad\text{ on }\overline{\Omega_{b}}.

3.

Non-degeneracy of the (fluid domain) ALE map.
There exists a positive constant
c_{3}>0
such that

0<c_{3}^{-1}\leq\mathcal{J}^{\omega_{h}}_{f}\leq c_{3},\quad\text{ on }\overline{\Omega_{f}},\quad|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}}_{f}|\leq c_{3},\quad\text{ on }\overline{\Omega_{f}}.

Remark 6.1
.

While we showed existence of a regularized interface weak solution in Section
5
, the result in Proposition
6.1
completes the proof of Theorem
5.1
on the approximate
h
-level problem, by verifying the final claim that the time of existence
T>0
of the regularized interface weak solution can be taken to be independent of the plate thickness parameter
0<h\leq 1
.

Proof.

We begin with the observation that since the regularization parameter
\delta>0
is fixed, we have that for every positive integer
k
and for a constant
C
independent of
h
(but potentially depending on
\delta
and
k
):

\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{k}(\Gamma))}\leq C\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{k+1}(\Omega_{b}))}\leq C(\delta,k)\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))}\leq C(\delta,k),

(95)

since the uniform energy estimates give a uniform bound on
\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
that is independent of
h
. We have also used the regularized kinematic coupling condition here, so that
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}|_{\Gamma}
. Therefore, we have that for
t\in[0,T]
for
T>0
to be determined and for
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}:=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}|_{\Gamma}
:

\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}(t,\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}\|_{H^{k}(\Gamma)}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}(s,\cdot)\|_{H^{k}(\Gamma)}ds\leq C(\delta,k)T.

(96)

Therefore, by choosing
T
sufficiently small and applying Proposition
3.1
, we obtain the results on the Lagrangian map for the plate
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}}_{\Gamma}
. We also obtain the results on the Lagrangian map for the Biot medium, since the above estimate (
95
) holds for all
k
, and hence, we can estimate for
0\leq t\leq T
:

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}^{\delta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{0}\|_{C^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}(t)-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{0}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega_{b})}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}(s)\|_{H^{3}(\Omega_{b})}ds\leq C(\delta)T.

Finally, we can estimate the ALE map for the fluid domain using results on traces and the estimate (
96
) applied for
k=2
to obtain the following estimate which is independent of
h
:

\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}(t)}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{0}}_{f}\|_{C^{1}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}(t)}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{0}}_{f}\|_{H^{5/2}(\Omega_{f})}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}(t)-\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\delta)T,

and hence by continuity, we can obtain the desired result on the ALE fluid domain map.
∎

Therefore, we have the following uniform boundedness results, where these bounds are uniform in the plate thickness
h>0
:

Proposition 6.2
.

For each fixed
\delta>0
, we have the following bounds which are uniform in the plate thickness
0<h\leq 1
:

•

\{\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}(t)))
and
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}(t)))
.

•

\{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))
and
W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
.

•

\{p_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
and
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))
.

•

\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{k}(\Gamma))
for all positive integers
k
.

•

\{\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{k}(\Gamma))
for all positive integers
k
.

•

\{h^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
.

•

\{h^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
.

•

\{h^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\}_{h>0}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
.

Hence, along a subsequence in
h\to 0
, we have the following convergences:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\eta},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{weakly-star in $L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))$ and weakly-star in $W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))$},

\displaystyle p_{h}\rightharpoonup p,\qquad

\displaystyle\text{weakly-star in $L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))$ and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))$}.

Proof.

This follows from the energy estimate in (
93
) which holds uniformly in
h
, since the initial energy
E_{0}
is uniformly bounded in
0<h\leq 1
. We, however, emphasize that this result can only be obtained from the energy estimate (
93
) as a result of the previous Proposition
6.1
, since some of the uniform estimates are on the moving Biot domain, whereas the uniform estimates above are given on the reference Biot domain. As an example, the energy estimate implies that there exists a constant
C
independent of
h
such that for all
0<h\leq 1
:

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}\left|\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}p_{h}\right|^{2}\leq C.

Since this estimate involves
\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}=\text{det}(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})
and the transformed gradient defined in (
21
):

\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}p_{h}=\nabla p_{h}\cdot(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})^{-1},

we can only obtain an estimate for
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{b}}|\nabla p_{h}|^{2}
that is uniform in
0<h\leq 1
using the previous geometric bounds in Proposition
6.1
, which hold uniformly in
0<h\leq 1
and
t\in[0,T]
, see (
60
).

We remark that the uniform bounds on the plate displacements and velocities
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\}_{h>0}
and
\{\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\}_{h>0}
follow from the uniform bounds on the Biot displacements
\{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}\}_{h>0}
in
W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
and the regularized kinematic coupling condition
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}|_{\Gamma}
, where we use the regularizing properties of spatial convolution and the trace inequality.
∎

With the uniform geometric bounds in Proposition
6.1
, we can similarly show the following uniform bounds on the trace of the fluid velocities and on the fluid velocities transferred to the fixed reference domain via the ALE map:

\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}:=\boldsymbol{u}_{h}\circ\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\omega_{h}}_{f},\qquad\text{ on }\Omega_{f}.

(97)

Proposition 6.3
.

The fluid velocities
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{h}
defined in (
97
) are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{f}))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{f}))
and the traces of the fluid velocities
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|_{\Gamma}
are uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
, independently of
0<h\leq 1
.

6.1
Compactness arguments in the limit as
h\to 0

Function spaces.
Because the weak formulation for the solutions with plate thickness
h
now satisfy a fully continuous-in-time weak formulation in Definition
5.1
rather than a semidiscrete weak formulation, we must work with different function spaces, though the underlying ideas behind the compactness arguments remain the same, as for the
N\to\infty
limit passage in Section
5.3
. We define these function spaces here, and identify the appropriate compact embedding result that we will invoke in the singular limit passage as
h\to 0
. For a Banach space
B
with associated norm
\|\cdot\|_{B}
, we define the fractional Sobolev space
W^{\alpha,p}(0,T;B)
to be the set of all functions
f\in L^{p}(0,T;B)
such that the following associated norm is finite:

\|f\|_{W^{\alpha,p}(0,T;B)}^{p}=\|f\|_{L^{p}(0,T;B)}^{p}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\|f(t)-f(s)\|_{B}}{|t-s|^{1+\alpha p}}dsdt.

(98)

We then obtain the following compact embedding result, which can be found in Theorem 2.1 in
[
36
]
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
B_{0}\subset\subset B\subset B_{1}
be reflexive Banach spaces, and let
0<\alpha<1
and
1<p<\infty
. Then,

L^{p}(0,T;B_{0})\cap W^{\alpha,p}(0,T;B_{1})\subset\subset L^{p}(0,T;B).

Compactness of the Biot displacement.
We can use Arzela-Ascoli to obtain the following convergence result for the Biot displacements, and by using the regularized interface condition, we can also obtain a corresponding convergence result for the interface displacements.

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists a limiting Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}
and a corresponding interface displacement
\boldsymbol{\omega}:=\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}|_{\Gamma}
, for which we have the following convergence results as
h\to 0
, along a subsequence:

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}\to\boldsymbol{\eta},\quad\text{ in }C(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b})),\qquad\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}\to\boldsymbol{\omega}\quad\text{ in }C(0,T;H^{k}(\Gamma)),

for all positive integers
k
.

Proof.

This follows from the uniform boundedness results (uniform in
h
), the following compact embedding
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))\cap W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))\subset\subset C(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
, and the regularized interface condition
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}|_{\Gamma}
combined with the regularizing properties of spatial convolution.
∎

Compactness for the Biot pore pressure.
By taking only the pressure test function to be nonzero in Definition
5.1
, we obtain that the pore pressure
p_{h}
must satisfy the following weak formulation for all times
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
, for all test functions
r\in V_{p}
, where
V_{p}
is defined in (
43
):

c_{0}\int_{\Omega_{b}}(p_{h}(t)-p_{h}(s))r=\alpha\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta_{h}^{\delta}}_{b}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}\cdot\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}r+\alpha\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}})r\\
-\kappa\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}p_{h}\cdot\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}r+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}((\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}})r.

(99)

Using this weak formulation, we can obtain the following strong convergence result for the pore pressures
p_{h}
in the limit as
h\to 0
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Along a subsequence in
h
,
p_{h}\to p
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))
.

Proof.

We use the compactness criterion stated in Proposition
6.4
. We first note that due to Proposition
6.2
, there exists a uniform constant
C
that is independent of
h
such that

\|p_{h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega_{b}))}\leq C.

Next, we claim that

\|p_{h}(t)-p_{h}(s)\|_{V_{p}^{\prime}}\leq C|t-s|^{1/2},\text{ for all $s,t\in[0,T]$},

(100)

for a constant
C
that is independent of
h
. To obtain this increment estimate, we can use the weak formulation for the increments of the pore pressure
p_{h}(t)-p_{h}(s)
in (
99
) and test by
r\in V_{p}
with
\|r\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
. We can estimate the resulting terms as follows:

•

For the first term, we observe that
\|\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))}
is uniformly bounded in
h
, and by the geometric bounds (uniform in
h
) in Proposition
6.1
on
\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}
and
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})^{-1}|
and the assumption that
\|\nabla r\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
, we deduce that
\displaystyle\left|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h}\cdot\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}r\right|\leq C|t-s|
.

•

The second term is also bounded by
\displaystyle\left|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}})r\right|\leq C|t-s|
once we make the following observations. First,
\|\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}
is uniformly bounded in
h
by Proposition
6.2
and
\|r\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C
by the trace inequality. Since
|\partial_{z}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}|\leq C
pointwise along
\Gamma
by the uniform high regularity bounds on
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{k}(\Omega))
for all positive integers
k
, we deduce that
|\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}}|\leq C
pointwise along
\Gamma
also, where the rescaled normal vector is defined in (
50
), which gives the desired estimate.

•

By the geometric bounds on
\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}
and
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\delta}_{h})^{-1}|
in Proposition
6.1
, the uniform bound on
\|\nabla p_{h}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))}
, and the fact that
\|\nabla r\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{b})}\leq 1
, we obtain

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{b}}\mathcal{J}^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}p_{h}\cdot\nabla^{\eta^{\delta}_{h}}_{b}r\right|\leq C|t-s|^{1/2}.

•

Finally, because
\boldsymbol{u}_{h}|_{\Gamma}
is bounded uniformly in
h
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
by Proposition
6.3
, and because
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}
is bounded uniformly in
h
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
by Proposition
6.2
, and
|\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}}|\leq C
pointwise along
\Gamma
, we obtain the final bound that

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}((\boldsymbol{u}_{h}-\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{h}})r\right|\leq C|t-s|^{1/2}.

Combining these estimates gives the desired increment estimate (
100
), from which we deduce that for any choice of
0<\alpha<1
and
1<p<\infty
such that
\alpha p<1/2
:

\|p_{h}\|_{W^{\alpha,p}(0,T;V_{p}^{\prime})}^{p}\leq\|p_{h}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(0,T;V_{p}^{\prime})}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}|t-s|^{-(\alpha p+1/2)}dsdt<\infty,

since
\|p_{h}\|_{L^{p}(0,T:V_{p}^{\prime})}\leq\|p_{h}\|_{L^{p}(0,T:L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))}\leq T^{1/p}\|p_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{b}))}
. The strong convergence result then follows from Proposition
6.4
using the chain of embeddings
H^{1}(\Omega_{b})\subset\subset L^{2}(\Omega_{b})\subset V_{p}^{\prime}
.
∎

Compactness for the Biot/interface velocity.
Next, we consider the limit of the Biot velocity and the plate velocity
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h})
for
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h}:=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{h}
. In the limit as
h\to 0
, there is no plate in the resulting problem, and we will hence instead refer to
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}
as the
interface velocity
, since in the limit as
h\to 0
,
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h}
will converge to the velocity of the regularized interface between the Biot medium and the fluid. We consider the following test space, as before in (
74
):

\mathcal{Q}_{v}=\{(\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{\varphi})\in V_{d}\times H^{2}(\Gamma):\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\delta}|_{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\varphi}\},

and we note (by using Definition
5.1
) that the Biot and interface velocities
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{h},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{h})
